the current report the four-output formula is Part~III's and AS's and FO's
proofs are a second route (\cref{fo:sec:answer}). The shipped audits are kept
verbatim.

\subsection{Primary published sources}

Both inspected Davies's arXiv version~2 of 17 October 2017 \cite{Davies2017}.
AS: HTML and PDF, with the relevant PDF pages, including the small-value table
and the concluding problems, visually inspected; Propositions~3--4, Section~3
($U_{a,b}$, the two-generation theorem, the generators attributed to Krawetz),
Theorem~3 and Corollary~3, Section~5 Problem~2 (printed p.~17; only the
four-output slice is claimed), and Table~3 (prior small values, and the
distinction between established and candidate optima; small numerical values
are not new). FO: the full parsed article and the displayed open-question page;
printed pp.~5--6 (Propositions~3--4), p.~7 ($U_{a,b}$ and the parity-adjusted
generators), p.~9 (Theorem~3), p.~12 (Corollary~3) and p.~17 (Problem~2 and the
small-value table); the version inspected has 18 printed pages. Neither claims
the generator, the orbit interpretation or the small lower-bound numbers as
new.

Holzer and K\"onig \cite{HolzerKoenig2004}: both import Theorem~8 through
Davies's statement; the original 2004 proof was not reconstructed or
independently audited, and FO checked the official publisher metadata. The
larger question of the maximum size of an arbitrary two-generated
transformation monoid is neither assumed nor resolved (FO). Davies's
attribution of the explicit generators to Krawetz \cite{Krawetz2003} is
retained; the thesis was not read in full (AS).

\subsection{Literature-search scope}

AS searched DFAO reversal, Davies's paper, the binary exact maximum and later
resolutions, and searched the repository for four-output follow-up work. FO
searched the full Davies title, ``DFAO reversal four'', and combinations of
``Davies'', ``reversal'', ``optimal'' and ``output''. Neither located a later
resolution. Neither is a systematic citation-index review, a survey of all
theses or unindexed manuscripts, or correspondence with the authors; a missing
search hit does not establish priority or prove the absence of a later
result, and neither manuscript makes a verified global-priority claim. FO's
claim of extension is specifically relative to the inspected repository
version.

\subsection{Dependency ledgers}

\emph{AS} (contribution claimed by its package): the exact four-output optimum
for every $n\ge4$, with the formula for $n\ge7$; an explicit elementary
structural exclusion for $n\ge26$ plus a complete certificate for
$4\le n\le25$; a strict structural margin greater than $ab$, an $ab/2$
stability radius and the specialized $24ab$ penalty; the exact missing-orbit
inventory and the extremal output-map count on the standard transition pair;
specialized exact sequence consequences, including the minimal order-15
recurrence. General ideas of asymptotics, recurrence extraction and
primitive-word enumeration are not claimed as new methods; the repository's
finite comparisons including four outputs are not claimed as new computations
of previously unknown numbers. AS's proof audit lists its boundary checks: the
closest-split formula is used only for $n\ge7$; the $n=6$ witness has two
triangular components, not one $C_6$; proper-orbit bounds do not imply generic
improper saturation; the $24ab$ penalty assumes injectivity on both cycles and
a proper initial output and is not claimed sharp; the count
$6P_2(a)P_2(b)$ concerns one fixed transition pair; a reverse-state search is
not an enumeration of all automata; and the two implementations are
independent only at the implementation level.

\emph{FO} (boundary against the predecessor): already present at its pin were
the coloring-orbit reduction, the collision-graph method and classification, a
universal structural upper bound, finite equality with Davies's construction
for $7\le n\le30$, $3\le k<n$, and the all-state three-output result with its
rigidity. FO's contribution relative to that snapshot: the all-state
four-output upper bound, the strict comparison selecting the nearest coprime
split, four-output rigidity, the four-color second-collision penalty,
secondary-scale stability, and the derived orbit inventory and exact
asymptotics. Graph coloring, the orbit reduction, primitive-word counting, the
biclique chromatic polynomial and the lower witness are not claimed as new;
the three-output proof is not used as an admitted premise. FO's proof audit
records that the certificate subtracts $mJ(r)$ or $\lcm(a,b)J(r)$, conservative
upper bounds on the permutation order, not a product of unrelated coloring
periods, and that $J(0)=J(1)=1$.

\subsection{Claims and nonclaims}

Part~IV's new contributions are the threshold-$26$ route with its
$2{,}082$- and $2{,}059$-entry certificates, the structural margin, the two
stability theorems, the missing-orbit inventory, the count of extremal output
maps, and the minimal order-15 recurrence with its reduced generating function.
The following are \emph{not} claimed:
\begin{itemize}
\item The four-output formula as a new theorem: it is Part~III's
 \cref{evo:thm:main} at $k=4$ for $n\ge7$, and Part~I's finite table for
 $n=4,5,6$; AS and FO give a second proof.
\item Anything for five or more outputs, the variable-output problem, growing
 $k$, or the diagonal: both manuscripts' claims are confined to four outputs.
\item A least threshold: $26$ is sufficient for the particular estimates, not
 optimal, and no conclusion below it is drawn by continuity or extrapolation
 (AS).
\item A new proof of Davies's lower construction or of the Holzer--K\"onig
 two-generation theorem; finite breadth-first searches do not replace it.
\item A classification of extremizers: \cref{fo:thm:rigidity} gives necessary
 conditions, (i)--(iii) are not sufficient
 (\cref{fo:fo:rem:notsufficient}); no classification of saturating singular
 generators or of extremal monoids (orbit extremality is weaker than
 monoid-size extremality); \cref{fo:as:thm:outputmaps} concerns one fixed
 transition pair and no other equivalence problem.
\item Sharpness of the penalties or of the stability radius; a second-largest
 value; a distance estimate between transition tables (FO); the near-maximum
 spectrum.
\item Novelty for the orbit reduction, the collision-graph method, the lower
 witness, primitive-word counting, standard Stirling and palette identities,
 or the small values $176$, $826$, $3526$ and $15{,}472$ (Part~I's tables,
 Davies's Table~3).
\item That the finite tables, sweeps or breadth-first searches prove any
 infinite statement.
\item Independent refereeing, priority, or machine-checked formalization.
\end{itemize}

\subsection{What remains open}

At $k=4$: the least analytic threshold and a computation-free proof for
$7\le n\le25$ (\cref{fo:q:computation}); a classification of saturating
singular letters (\cref{fo:q:saturate,fo:fo:q:monoids}); the optimal stability
radius, the second-largest value and the near-maximum spectrum
(\cref{fo:q:stability,fo:fo:q:spectrum}); sharp penalties for several
collisions (\cref{fo:q:collisions}); word-length bounds for saturation and the
enumeration of inequivalent extremizers (\cref{fo:as:q:words,fo:as:q:enumerate});
formal verification (\cref{fo:fo:q:formal}). Beyond $k=4$ the open questions
are Part~III's.

\subsection{Where the merge had to choose}\label{fo:sec:merge}
\begin{itemize}
\item \emph{Role.} Both manuscripts re-derive Part~III's $k=4$ case, which
 neither had read. Part~IV therefore prints the formula as Part~III's (and
 Part~I's for $n\le6$) and the manuscripts' proofs of it as a second route
 (\cref{fo:thm:main} and \cref{fo:sec:answer}), instead of as a new theorem;
 the batch-43 placement (\texttt{faef2ed2a}) records the same decision.
\item \emph{Placement.} Part~IV follows Part~III's last section and precedes
 Part~I's appendices, which contain no numbered equations, tables, figures or
 theorem-like items; no number of Parts~I--III changed (compared in the
 \texttt{.aux} files of the committed and the new build).
\item \emph{Base.} AS (weaker hypothesis $n\ge4$, proved minimality of the
 recurrence, the output count); its order of sections is kept, and FO's
 material is inserted where it belongs. FO's proofs that differ from AS's are
 kept as marked second routes (\cref{fo:fo:lem:eliminate,fo:fo:lem:threshold,fo:fo:prop:large},
 the second proof of \cref{fo:thm:rigidity}, FO's witness); FO-only results
 keep FO's hypotheses (\cref{fo:fo:prop:penalty} without properness,
 \cref{fo:fo:thm:stability} for $n\ge26$). FO's proofs were re-read for hidden
 uses of properness, the hypothesis AS's penalty needs: none was found; its penalty uses only the
 two cross pairs, and its stability theorem only $\defi<7\cdot2^n$.
\item \emph{Duplicates, printed once.} The orbit reduction, the collision
 obstruction, the classification, the cycle and biclique counts (both
 manuscripts; Part~I, Part~III); the unmixed cases (both; Part~I, Part~III's
 \cref{evo:lem:easy}); the closest split and balancing (both; Part~III's
 \cref{evo:cor:split}); the upper bound for $n\ge7$ and its witness (both;
 Part~III); the rigidity theorem (both, and Part~III's \cref{evo:thm:rigidity}
 in weaker form); the one-edge count (FO; Part~III); the inventory (AS's and
 FO's formulas, one theorem); the output-map count (AS's theorem, FO's remark);
 the residue asymptotics (both; Part~III's \cref{evo:thm:evenasymptotic} as an
 expansion); the order-15 operator (both: AS proves minimality, FO annihilation);
 the generating-function data (both, identical numbers).
\item \emph{Tables.} AS's and FO's certificate tables are printed as one
 (\cref{fo:tab:finite}; the margins agree, the entry counts differ by FO's
 nonsurjective entry); the column $R_2(n,4)$ is AS's \texttt{maximum} field
 of \path{data/07-all-state-finite_summary.csv}. AS's and FO's $n=7$
 inventories are printed as one table (\cref{fo:ex:inventory7}).
\item \emph{Questions.} The $16$ questions are printed as $11$, merged where
 they ask the same thing, with both sources' comments;
 \cref{fo:q:next} is printed as answered by Part~III (except its uniform
 part), and each question names its Part~III counterpart.
\item \emph{Order and pieces.} AS's verification section and appendix on
 finite algorithms, and FO's reproducibility section and compact enumerator,
 are merged into \cref{fo:sec:verification}; the two formalization plans are
 \cref{fo:sec:formal}; FO's literature check and the four audits are this
 section; AS's appendix on generating-function data is in
 \cref{fo:sec:recurrence}; the two title-page status statements are the
 status box of \cref{fo:sec:source}; the two conclusions are
 \cref{fo:sec:conclusion}.
\item \emph{Notation.} All renamings are in \cref{fo:tab:notation}; in
 particular $A,B\to a_n,b_n$ (or $a,b$), $D_n\to D^*_4(n)$ and $D_4(n)$ (Part~II's
 $D_n$ is the three-output deficit), $\mathcal B(a,b)\to\Bip_4(a,b)$, FO's cycles $L,R\to
 X,Y$, $h(t),g(t)\to\psi(t),4\psi(t)$, FO's $E(a,b)\to\Omega^{\mathrm e}_4$ and
 AS's $E(u,v)\to\mathcal C_{u,v}$ (the two manuscripts' $E$ collide with each
 other), FO's $N_{u,v}\to o_{u,v}$, $\chi_D,\mathcal A(E)\to\chi_4(\mathsf S)$,
 FO's advance coefficients $a_j\to\alpha_j$, FO's constant $C\to\kappa$. No

\appendix
\section{Explicit small witnesses}\label{app:witnesses}

The following arrays use state labels $0,\ldots,n-1$. Except for the
$(5,4)$ witness, the permutation is the full cycle
$p=(1,2,\ldots,n-1,0)$. The singular arrays used are
\[
\begin{array}{c|l}
 n&s\\\hline
3&(0,0,2)\\
4&(0,0,3,2)\\
5&(0,0,3,4,2)\\
6&(0,3,0,4,5,1).
\end{array}
\]
Suitable output arrays are
\[
\begin{array}{cc|l|r}
n&k&\tau&|\tau\langle p,s\rangle|\\\hline
3&3&(0,1,2)&24\\
4&3&(0,1,0,2)&67\\
4&4&(0,1,2,3)&176\\
5&3&(0,1,0,1,2)&218\\
5&5&(0,1,2,3,4)&2110\\
6&3&(0,0,1,1,2,2)&699\\
6&4&(0,0,1,1,2,3)&3526\\
6&5&(0,0,1,2,3,4)&12031\\
6&6&(0,1,2,3,4,5)&32262.
\end{array}
\]
For $(n,k)=(5,4)$ use instead
\[
 p=(1,0,3,4,2),\qquad s=(2,1,2,3,0),\qquad
 \tau=(0,1,2,3,3),
\]
whose orbit has size $826$. Every listed machine is accessible from state
$0$ and is output-minimal; both properties are checked in the supplied code.
The arrays are explicit witnesses only. Their use does not assert that every
machine with the same collision graph has the same orbit size.

\section{Evaluating the finite bounds}\label{app:bounds}

The Stirling numbers needed in \eqref{eq:bipoly} are computed by the recurrence
\[
 \stirling{u}{i}=i\stirling{u-1}{i}+\stirling{u-1}{i-1},
\]
with $\stirling{0}{0}=1$ and all impossible indices zero. This follows by
considering whether the last element joins one of $i$ existing blocks or
forms a new singleton block. Thus no symbolic algebra package is needed.

The possible permutation orders can be enumerated recursively through
nondecreasing cycle lengths. If the remaining number of points is $r$ and
all remaining parts must be at least $j$, choose a first part $i\ge j$ and
combine $i$ by least common multiple with an order from a partition of
$r-i$ having parts at least $i$. Retaining only distinct resulting orders
substantially reduces repeated work. The base case for zero remaining
points is the singleton set $\{1\}$.

A direct evaluator then performs the following operations:
\begin{lstlisting}
orders[r] = orders obtained from every partition of r
H(r, t)   = max(lcm(t, o) for o in orders[r])

candidates = [nonsurjective_gap, permutation_gap,
              two_singular_gap]

for m in 2..n:
    for each proper positive divisor d of m:
        add C_(m/d)(k)^d * k^(n-m) - H(n-m, m)

for a in 1..n-1:
    for b in a..n-a:
        d = gcd(a, b)
        r = n-a-b
        period = H(r, lcm(a,b))
        if k == 3 and d == 1:
            period = max(H(r,a), H(r,b))
        add B_k(a/d,b/d)^d * k^r - period

upper = k^n - min(candidates)
\end{lstlisting}

The lower evaluator independently scans coprime splits $1<\ell<m$ with
$\ell+m=n$ and maximizes \eqref{eq:lower}. All arithmetic is exact. The data
file retains both results rather than storing only a Boolean equality flag.
It therefore records the actual extremal values, including numbers far
larger than those feasible for literal coloring-orbit enumeration.

\section{A complete three-state reachability certificate}\label{app:words}

For the maps of \eqref{eq:n3}, a displayed word $x_1\cdots x_r$ denotes
successive pullbacks, so the resulting coloring is
$\tau\circ x_1\circ\cdots\circ x_r$. The empty word is $\varepsilon$.
Each row can be checked by direct tuple substitution; no search program is
needed to verify the certificate once this table is given. This is the one
place in the article where an exact orbit size is established entirely by
hand.

\begin{center}
\begin{tabular}{cc@{\qquad\qquad}cc}
\toprule
Coloring & Word & Coloring & Word\\
\midrule
$(0,0,0)$ & $bab$ & $(1,1,2)$ & $aabaab$\\
$(0,0,1)$ & $abaab$ & $(1,2,0)$ & $a$\\
$(0,0,2)$ & $b$ & $(1,2,1)$ & $aabaaba$\\
$(0,1,0)$ & $abaaba$ & $(1,2,2)$ & $aabaa$\\
$(0,1,1)$ & $abaa$ & $(2,0,0)$ & $baa$\\
$(0,1,2)$ & $\varepsilon$ & $(2,0,1)$ & $aa$\\
$(0,2,0)$ & $ba$ & $(2,0,2)$ & $baaba$\\
$(0,2,2)$ & $baabaa$ & $(2,1,1)$ & $aabaabaa$\\
$(1,0,0)$ & $abaabaa$ & $(2,1,2)$ & $aaba$\\
$(1,0,1)$ & $aba$ & $(2,2,0)$ & $baab$\\
$(1,1,0)$ & $ab$ & $(2,2,1)$ & $aab$\\
$(1,1,1)$ & $abab$ & $(2,2,2)$ & $aabab$\\
\bottomrule
\end{tabular}
\end{center}

There are $24$ distinct colorings in the table. Combined with the triangle
obstruction excluding the other three, this proves the exact orbit size
without relying on a claim that a program has examined every word.
The same table is stored in \texttt{results/sharp\_n3\_orbit.json} as $24$
pairs of a coloring and a word.

\section{Proof audit: points where the argument could go wrong}\label{app:audit}

The main proof has a short dependency chain:
\[
 \begin{gathered}
 \text{collision pair}\ \Longrightarrow\ \text{three-colorable orbital graph}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{surjective proper }k\text{-coloring},\\
 \text{fiber-partition stabilizer in a cyclic group}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ |\Orb_\tau\cap\mathcal R(c)|\le g(k),\\
 \text{rightmost singular factor}
 \ \Longrightarrow\ \text{proper reachable states use only the permutation},\\
 \text{these three statements}\ \Longrightarrow\ k!-g(k)\text{ missing states}.
 \end{gathered}
\]
The cases with two singular or two invertible generators are handled
separately and do not require the cyclic graph argument. The following points
are stated explicitly because omitting any one of them can make an attractive
informal argument incomplete or incorrect. This list merges two separately
written audit lists, and every item of each is retained: the two overlap on
the rightmost-factor and surjectivity points, but each carried items the
other lacked, so a shorter list would silently drop a genuine warning.

\textbf{The side of composition.} A word containing a singular map must be
factored as $u\circ s\circ p^j$, using its \emph{rightmost} singular factor.
The collapsed input pair is then $p^{-j}\{x,y\}$. Factoring on the other
side without adjusting the argument would not identify a fixed graph edge.

\textbf{Surjectivity in the relabeling argument.} From
$\sigma\circ c=\rho\circ c$ one can conclude $\sigma=\rho$ only on the
image of $c$. The construction therefore refines a proper coloring until it
uses every output label before invoking the symmetric group; this is also
what makes the $k!$ relabelings of that coloring pairwise distinct, so that
the counting argument is not silently overcounting.

\textbf{The fiber partition is stabilized setwise, not blockwise.} In the
formulation of \cref{rem:setwise}, $H$ consists of the group elements that
permute the fibers of $c$ among themselves. Requiring each block to be
preserved individually would give a smaller subgroup, a trivial induced
color permutation, and a bound that proves nothing. Conversely, allowing
arbitrary set maps rather than elements of the acting group would give no
subgroup at all.

\textbf{A cyclic component of size two is a single edge.} When the quotient
length is $2$, the component of the collision-orbit graph consists of one
undirected edge, not a two-edge multigraph on the same pair. The chromatic
factor is $k(k-1)$, which is what \eqref{eq:cyclepoly} returns at $L=2$;
treating it as a genuine $2$-cycle with two parallel edges would be a
modeling error even though the count happens to agree.

\textbf{Different actions commute; arbitrary output permutations do not.}
Equation \eqref{eq:commuteactions} is associativity of postcomposition and
precomposition. The contradiction is that putting two noncommuting output
permutations in one cyclic input orbit would force them to commute on a
surjective coloring. No commutativity of all function composition is
assumed.

\textbf{Distinct colorings versus distinct words.} The orbit size counts
functions $Q\to\D$, not products or input words. The subgroup-image proof
and all BFS implementations deduplicate colorings explicitly.

\textbf{Proper colorings need not be surjective in the counting bound.}
The chromatic polynomial counts all proper maps into a fixed $k$-symbol
alphabet. This is exactly the set excluded by singular-containing words.
Surjectivity is needed for the factorial relabeling argument, not for
\eqref{eq:chromaticbound}.

\textbf{Inaccessible proper colorings do not make improper ones accessible.}
The argument shows only that proper colorings are unreachable through words
containing the singular letter. It says nothing about which improper
colorings occur, and in particular does not license the converse reading that
everything not excluded by the graph is reached. That converse is the
saturation property discussed in \cref{sec:structural}, and it has to be
established separately for any claimed equality.

\textbf{A graph upper bound does not create a witness.} Knowing that at most
$P_G(k)-T$ colorings are lost gives only an upper bound on orbit size. Lower
witnesses and their reachability must be supplied separately. The finite
optimality statements explicitly combine a universal upper calculation with
a lower construction or a complete orbit search of an explicit machine.
